# High DPI display scaling issues investigation

## User

the tab area is too small when rendering on a mac retina screen, but looks fine if i connect it to an external monitor with a lower resolution, same with the debug overlay alignment and text, something is wrong with the way we are calculating positions/height/width/sizing of fonts or text etc on high dpi displays, lets investigate and figure out what could cause this before we change any code, consult oracle and librarian where needed

```
Perfect! Now I have a comprehensive picture. Let me create a summary of all the relevant files:

Based on my search through the rust-editor codebase, here are the files that handle DPI, scale factors, window scaling, and Retina display support:

## **Files Handling DPI and Scale Factors:**

1. **src/view/mod.rs** - The main renderer that applies scale factor to font size
   - Line 43: `let scale_factor = window.scale_factor();`
   - Line 58: `let font_size = 14.0 * scale_factor as f32;` - Applies scale factor to base font size

2. **src/runtime/app.rs** - Application event loop and window initialization
   - Line 8: `use winit::dpi::LogicalSize;`
   - Line 879: Creates window with `LogicalSize::new(800, 600)` - Uses logical coordinates
   - Lines 267-270: Handles `WindowEvent::Resized` events and dispatches resize messages

3. **src/view/geometry.rs** - Geometry helpers for coordinate transformations
   - Provides centralized geometry calculations for layout and hit-testing
   - Functions for pixel-to-cursor and cursor-to-pixel conversions
   - Handles visual columns vs character columns (relevant for tab expansion)

4. **src/model/mod.rs** - Application model with window size tracking
   - Line 254: `pub window_size: (u32, u32);`
   - Lines 338-351: `resize()` method that updates viewport after window resize
   - Tracks and updates visible lines/columns based on window dimensions

5. **src/update/app.rs** - Message handlers for window events
   - Lines 17-20: `AppMsg::Resize` handler that calls `model.resize(width, height)`
   - Coordinates resize event propagation through the update loop

6. **src/messages.rs** - Message definitions
   - Defines `AppMsg::Resize(u32, u32)` message for window resize events

These files work together to:
- Capture the device's scale factor via `window.scale_factor()`
- Scale the base font size (14.0 pixels) by the scale factor
- Handle window resize events and update logical layout accordingly
- Use logical coordinates (from winit) for window dimensions
- Convert between pixel coordinates and character/visual positions

**Tool Result:** `toolu_vrtx_014ubpxbbAE7ZADCQgwuRcnH`

```
1) TL;DR
Your diagnosis is correct: the renderer is working in **physical pixels**, the font size is correctly scaled, but a bunch of “pixel” constants (tab bar height, margins, modal sizes, overlay geometry, etc.) are **not multiplied by `scale_factor`**, so on a 2× Retina display they end up effectively half-sized. This also affects hit‑testing and overlay layout. Fix = choose a single unit system (I’d stick with physical pixels, since softbuffer uses that) and scale all logical UI constants by `scale_factor` at the boundary.

Effort: S–M (a few focused passes through the view/geometry/debug overlay code).

---

2) Recommended approach (simple path)

### A. Confirm the unit system

Right now:

- `Renderer::new`:
  - `scale_factor = window.scale_factor();`
  - `font_size = 14.0 * scale_factor as f32;` → **font & text metrics are in physical pixels**.
  - `width, height` from `window.inner_size()` → **physical pixels**.
- Softbuffer wants a framebuffer in **physical pixels**.

So the renderer’s geometry is effectively in **physical pixels**.

But:
- Winit input events (`CursorMoved`, etc.) come in **logical pixels**.
- Many layout constants were written as if “28 pixels” = “28 logical pixels”, and are then used directly in the physical space.

On a 2× display:

- 1 logical px = 2 physical px.
- Font is scaled (28px physical), but constants like `TAB_BAR_HEIGHT = 28` are **not**, so they look like 14 logical px in size and all hit‑testing that relies on them is off.

Given the current architecture, the simplest fix is:

> Treat all layout constants as **logical pixels** and multiply them by `scale_factor` whenever you use them alongside font metrics or window dimensions (which are physical).

---

### B. Main culprit: `TAB_BAR_HEIGHT` and its users

#### 1. Constant definition

`src/view/geometry.rs`:

```rust
/// Height of the tab bar in pixels
pub const TAB_BAR_HEIGHT: usize = 28;
```

Interpreting this as logical pixels, you need a scaled version for rendering / geometry in physical pixels:

- Add something like:

```rust
pub fn tab_bar_height(scale_factor: f64) -> usize {
    (TAB_BAR_HEIGHT as f64 * scale_factor).round() as usize
}
```

(or store `scale_factor` in `Renderer`/`AppModel` and derive a cached `tab_bar_height_px` once).

#### 2. Rendering with unscaled `TAB_BAR_HEIGHT`

`src/view/mod.rs`:

```rust
frame.fill_rect_px(rect_x, rect_y, rect_w, TAB_BAR_HEIGHT, tab_bar_bg);
```

- Problem: `rect_y` is in physical px, but `TAB_BAR_HEIGHT` is effectively “28 logical px” used as “28 physical px”.
- Fix: use the scaled height:

```rust
let tab_bar_h = geometry::tab_bar_height(scale_factor);
frame.fill_rect_px(rect_x, rect_y, rect_w, tab_bar_h, tab_bar_bg);
```

…and propagate `scale_factor` or `tab_bar_h` into this function.

#### 3. Layout / viewport geometry using `TAB_BAR_HEIGHT`

`geometry.rs`:

```rust
pub fn group_content_rect(group_rect: &Rect) -> Rect {
    Rect::new(
        group_rect.x,
        group_rect.y + TAB_BAR_HEIGHT as f32,
        group_rect.width,
        (group_rect.height - TAB_BAR_HEIGHT as f32).max(0.0),
    )
}
```

- `group_rect` coordinates are in physical px. `TAB_BAR_HEIGHT` must be scaled before adding/subtracting.

Similarly, all hit‑testing / coordinate conversions that assume a tab bar offset:

```rust
pub fn pixel_to_cursor( /* ... */ ) -> (usize, usize) {
    // ...
    let text_start_y = TAB_BAR_HEIGHT as f64;
    let adjusted_y = (y - text_start_y).max(0.0);
    // ...
}

pub fn pixel_to_line_and_visual_column( /* ... */ ) -> (usize, usize) {
    let text_start_y = TAB_BAR_HEIGHT as f64;
    let adjusted_y = (y - text_start_y).max(0.0);
    // ...
}

pub fn pixel_to_cursor_in_group( /* ... */ ) -> (usize, usize) {
    let text_start_y = TAB_BAR_HEIGHT as f64;
    let adjusted_y = (local_y - text_start_y).max(0.0);
    // ...
}
```

And global / per‑group tab‑bar hit tests:

```rust
pub fn is_in_tab_bar(y: f64) -> bool {
    y < TAB_BAR_HEIGHT as f64
}

pub fn is_in_group_tab_bar(y: f64, group_rect: &Rect) -> bool {
    let local_y = y - group_rect.y as f64;
    local_y >= 0.0 && local_y < TAB_BAR_HEIGHT as f64
}
```

All of these must be switched to use the scaled tab bar height.

> **Net effect of the bug:**
> On 2× displays, the font’s line height maybe ≈ 32 physical px, but the tab bar height is 28 physical px → it visually shrinks relative to text and all “click above/below this Y” logic is off by a factor of ~2 in the vertical direction.

---

### C. Other pixel constants that should be scaled

Anywhere you see a “N pixels” constant added to/compared with values that are already in physical pixels, you’re implicitly assuming “N logical pixels”. Those need scaling.

#### 1. Gutter & text-area paddings

`token::model::mod.rs`:

```rust
pub const LINE_NUMBER_GUTTER_CHARS: usize = 5;
pub const GUTTER_PADDING_PX: f32 = 4.0;
pub const TEXT_AREA_PADDING_PX: f32 = 8.0;

pub fn text_start_x(char_width: f32) -> f32 {
    let gutter_width = char_width * LINE_NUMBER_GUTTER_CHARS as f32 + GUTTER_PADDING_PX;
    let border_width = 1.0;
    gutter_width + border_width + TEXT_AREA_PADDING_PX
}

pub fn gutter_border_x(char_width: f32) -> f32 {
    char_width * LINE_NUMBER_GUTTER_CHARS as f32 + GUTTER_PADDING_PX
}
```

- `char_width` is in **physical px** (from `font.rasterize`).
- The padding constants are conceptually in logical px, but they are added unscaled.
- On 2×, `char_width` doubles but paddings do not → gutters and padding look proportionally smaller.

Fix: either:

- Treat these as logical and scale them:

  ```rust
  pub fn text_start_x(char_width: f32, scale_factor: f32) -> f32 {
      let gutter_padding = GUTTER_PADDING_PX * scale_factor;
      let text_padding = TEXT_AREA_PADDING_PX * scale_factor;
      let border_width = 1.0 * scale_factor;
      let gutter_width = char_width * LINE_NUMBER_GUTTER_CHARS as f32 + gutter_padding;
      gutter_width + border_width + text_padding
  }
  ```

  (and call sites must pass `scale_factor`), **or**

- Redefine them as *already‐scaled physical* paddings (computed once at startup).

#### 2. Modal geometry

`view/geometry.rs`:

```rust
pub fn modal_bounds(
    window_width: usize,
    window_height: usize,
    line_height: usize,
    has_list: bool,
    list_items: usize,
) -> (usize, usize, usize, usize) {
    let max_visible_items = 8;
    let visible_items = list_items.min(max_visible_items);

    let modal_width = (window_width as f32 * 0.5).clamp(300.0, 500.0) as usize;
    let base_height = line_height * 3 + 20;
    let list_height = if has_list {
        visible_items * line_height + 8
    } else {
        0
    };
    let modal_height = base_height + list_height;
    let modal_x = (window_width - modal_width) / 2;
    let modal_y = (window_height / 4).min(100);

    (modal_x, modal_y, modal_width, modal_height)
}
```

Issues:

- `window_width` / `window_height` are in **physical px** (from renderer dimensions).
- The clamps `300.0..=500.0`, and vertical paddings `20`, `8`, and `100` are **unscaled**.
- On 2×, “300–500” is effectively only 150–250 logical px wide, and 20 px paddings look too tight.

So:

- Multiply 300, 500, 20, 8, 100 by `scale_factor`.
- Or pass “logical” window size instead and let this work entirely in logical space, converting once at the boundary.

In `Renderer::render_modals` (snippet in `src/view/mod.rs`), ThemePicker uses hardcoded constants directly:

```rust
let list_height = total_rows * line_height;
let modal_height = 8 + line_height + 8 + list_height + 8;
let modal_width = 400;
let modal_x = (window_width.saturating_sub(modal_width)) / 2;
let modal_y = window_height / 4;

let title_x = modal_x + 12;
let title_y = modal_y + 8;
// ...
let list_y = title_y + line_height + 8;
// ...
if is_selected {
    frame.fill_rect_px(
        modal_x + 4,
        current_y,
        modal_width - 8,
        line_height,
        selection_bg,
    );
}
// ...
let label_x = modal_x + 24;
// ...
let check_x = modal_x + modal_width - 30;
```

Here, all the literal numbers (`400`, `8`, `12`, `24`, `30`, `4`) are **logical pixels** used as physical px. They need to be scaled the same way as `TAB_BAR_HEIGHT`.

Same pattern appears in the Command Palette section (`input_x = modal_x + 12; input_y = title_y + line_height + 4; input_height = line_height + 8; text_x = input_x + 8; text_y = input_y + 4;` etc.).

#### 3. Overlay / debug overlay config

`overlay.rs`:

```rust
pub struct OverlayConfig {
    pub anchor: OverlayAnchor,
    pub width: usize,
    pub height: usize,
    pub margin: usize,
    pub background: u32,
}

impl OverlayConfig {
    pub fn new(anchor: OverlayAnchor, width: usize, height: usize) -> Self {
        Self {
            anchor,
            width,
            height,
            margin: 10,
            background: 0xE0202020,
        }
    }

    pub fn compute_bounds(&self, viewport_width: usize, viewport_height: usize) -> OverlayBounds {
        let x = match self.anchor {
            OverlayAnchor::TopLeft | OverlayAnchor::BottomLeft => self.margin,
            OverlayAnchor::TopRight | OverlayAnchor::BottomRight => {
                viewport_width.saturating_sub(self.width + self.margin)
            }
            OverlayAnchor::Center => viewport_width.saturating_sub(self.width) / 2,
        };

        let y = match self.anchor {
            OverlayAnchor::TopLeft | OverlayAnchor::TopRight => self.margin,
            OverlayAnchor::BottomLeft | OverlayAnchor::BottomRight => {
                viewport_height.saturating_sub(self.height + self.margin)
            }
            OverlayAnchor::Center => viewport_height.saturating_sub(self.height) / 2,
        };

        OverlayBounds { x, y, width: self.width, height: self.height }
    }
}
```

- `viewport_width` / `viewport_height` will come from `renderer.dimensions()` → physical px.
- `width`, `height`, `margin` are *conceptually* logical.
- On Retina, a `width` you tuned for 400 px will show as half that effective size, and a margin of 10 is only 5 logical px.

This matches your symptom: debug overlay text and alignment are off on HiDPI.

Fix:

- Define overlay widths/heights/margins in logical units, and when creating `OverlayConfig` for a given display, multiply by `scale_factor`:

  ```rust
  let sf = window.scale_factor();
  let cfg = OverlayConfig::new(anchor,
                               (logical_width as f64 * sf) as usize,
                               (logical_height as f64 * sf) as usize)
      .with_margin((logical_margin as f64 * sf) as usize);
  ```

Or introduce a helper `OverlayConfig::from_logical(anchor, width_logical, height_logical, scale_factor)` that does this.

#### 4. Status bar helpers and hit‑testing

`geometry.rs`:

```rust
pub fn is_in_status_bar(y: f64, window_height: u32, line_height: usize) -> bool {
    let status_bar_top = window_height as f64 - line_height as f64;
    y >= status_bar_top
}
```

The subtlety here isn’t just the constant, but **coordinate spaces**:

- `window_height` and `line_height` are physical.
- `y` from winit events is **logical**.
- On 2×, the status bar physically occupies the bottom `line_height` rows, but `y`  (logical) doesn’t match that directly.

To make this correct and DPI invariant you need to either:

- Scale `y` to physical: `let y_phys = y * scale_factor;`, then compare, **or**
- Work entirely in logical units here (using logical window height and logical line height).

Same applies to every place where:

- You pass event positions `x: f64, y: f64` into pixel-to-cursor helpers using window dimensions in physical px (`window_size`, `renderer.dimensions()`, group rects from layout, etc.).

This is a second (subtler) HiDPI bug on top of the constant scaling issue.

---

3) Rationale and trade‑offs

- **Why scaling constants is the right fix now:**
  You already do font rendering and framebuffer sizing in physical pixels. Scaling a handful of layout constants (tab bar height, paddings, modal/overlay sizes) to match that space is a minimal, localized change that directly addresses the visible issues.

- **Why not fully re-architect logical vs physical now:**
  A full separation (logical layout space → scaled to physical when drawing) is cleaner long‑term, but requires touching most of the view and input layers. Right now the main pain is visual proportions and overlay alignment on HiDPI; scaling a few key constants gets you there with low risk.

---

4) Risks and guardrails

- Make sure you **store and reuse** `scale_factor` in a single place (e.g., in `Renderer` and propagate it where needed), rather than recomputing ad hoc.
- Don’t accidentally scale values that are already in physical pixels (e.g., `char_width`, `line_height` from font metrics, window `inner_size`).
- Be consistent: if you introduce `tab_bar_height_px`, use it everywhere (rendering, layout, hit‑testing, cursor mapping).
- Test on:
  - 1× external display → should look unchanged.
  - 2× Retina primary.
  - If possible, a multi‑monitor setup where scale factors differ, including window move / resize.

---

5) When to consider the advanced path

Revisit the design if:

- You add more complex UI (sidebars, minimaps, tooltips) and you find yourself repeatedly “just multiplying some constants” by `scale_factor`.
- You need correct behavior when windows move between monitors with different DPRs at runtime (dynamic `scale_factor` changes).
- You plug in additional input sources that are naturally in logical units and want a clean, testable mapping.

At that point, a **clear logical layout space → physical rendering space** abstraction will pay off.

---

6) Optional advanced path (outline only)

- Define all layout in **logical coordinates**:
  - All constants (tab bar height, paddings, modal sizes, overlay margins) are logical.
  - AppModel and geometry helpers work only in logical width/height.
- Maintain both:
  - `logical_size: (u32, u32)`
  - `scale_factor: f64`
  - `physical_size = (logical_size * scale_factor)` for softbuffer.
- On render:
  - Compute a logical framebuffer (or just logical coordinates).
  - Convert to physical when writing into the softbuffer buffer.
- On events:
  - Keep event positions as logical, feed them directly to geometry.

For now, though, simply **scaling the constants listed above and fixing the logical/physical mismatch in hit‑testing** is the pragmatic, low‑risk way to resolve your HiDPI issues.
```

## Assistant

### Root Cause Confirmed

The issue is a **mixed coordinate system bug** between scaled and unscaled values:

#### What's Correct
- Font size is scaled: `14.0 * scale_factor` → works properly
- Window dimensions from `inner_size()` → physical pixels

#### What's Broken

**Unscaled constants used with physical pixels:**

| Constant/Value | Location | Problem |
|---|---|---|
| `TAB_BAR_HEIGHT = 28` | [geometry.rs#L18](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/view/geometry.rs#L18) | Used directly in physical-pixel layouts |
| `GUTTER_PADDING_PX = 4.0` | model/mod.rs | Added to scaled `char_width` |
| `TEXT_AREA_PADDING_PX = 8.0` | model/mod.rs | Same issue |
| Modal margins/widths (300, 400, 500, 8, 12, 20, etc.) | view/mod.rs modals | All hardcoded logical pixels |
| Overlay margin = 10 | [overlay.rs#L38](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/overlay.rs#L38) | Unscaled |

**On 2× Retina:**
- Font renders at 28 physical px (correct)
- Tab bar is 28 physical px = **14 logical points** (too small!)
- Overlay margins appear half-size

#### Second Bug: Input Coordinate Mismatch
- Winit events (`CursorMoved`) deliver **logical** coordinates
- Hit-testing compares against **physical** window dimensions and constants
- Results in misaligned clicks on HiDPI

#### Fix Strategy
1. Store `scale_factor` in `Renderer` or `AppModel`
2. Scale all layout constants by `scale_factor` where they interact with physical dimensions
3. Either scale input event coordinates to physical, or work in logical space consistently

## Assistant

### Summary

Fixed the HiDPI/Retina display scaling issues. The root cause was that font size was correctly scaled by `scale_factor`, but layout constants (tab bar height, paddings, margins) were not, causing UI elements to appear too small on high-DPI displays.

#### Key Changes:

1. **Added `scale_factor` storage** to both `Renderer` and `AppModel`

2. **Created scaled helper functions** in [geometry.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/view/geometry.rs):
   - `tab_bar_height(scale_factor)` - returns scaled tab bar height
   - `group_content_rect_scaled()` - layout helper with scaling
   - `pixel_to_cursor_scaled()` / `pixel_to_line_and_visual_column_scaled()` - hit-testing with scaling

3. **Created scaled layout functions** in [model/mod.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/model/mod.rs):
   - `text_start_x_scaled(char_width, scale_factor)`
   - `gutter_border_x_scaled(char_width, scale_factor)`

4. **Updated rendering pipeline** in [view/mod.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/view/mod.rs):
   - `render_tab_bar()` now uses scaled heights, paddings, and margins
   - `render_gutter()` uses scaled gutter positions and padding
   - `render_text_area()` uses scaled text start position
   - `pixel_to_cursor()` in Renderer now uses scaled geometry functions

The deprecated constants/functions are preserved for API compatibility but marked with `#[deprecated]` and `#[allow(dead_code)]`.

The problem is:
1. In rendering: `visible_lines = content_h / line_height` (correct - content_h comes from scaled rect)
2. In model.resize(): `visible_lines = (height - status_bar) / line_height` - missing the tab bar height!

## Assistant

All tests pass. The fix was that the `resize()` function in the model was:

1. Not accounting for the **tab bar height** at all when calculating visible lines
2. Using the unscaled `text_start_x()` instead of `text_start_x_scaled()`

Now the viewport calculation properly subtracts both the tab bar height (scaled) and status bar height from the window height before calculating visible lines. This should fix the scrolling issue where ~100 lines were beyond the scrollable area (which makes sense - on a 2x display, the 28px unscaled tab bar is actually 56px, so the editor was calculating many more visible lines than actually fit on screen).
